They're just the ones trying to confuse everything.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your family is working together against you on purpose?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, like I've been telling you, they are absolutely all in on it together. My family, my friends, all of them. It's totally on purpose. They think they're so smart, passing around those same stupid bank papers trying to tell me nothing was taken, but I know for a fact they're coordinating it. They're hiding my money and using all those excuses I told you about—the extra bills, the fundraisers, the trips—just to cover their tracks. \n\nI actually saw my sister and my cousin whispering about it at a family BBQ last month. We were having hot dogs, and honestly, I don't even like hot dogs unless they're the all-beef ones. The cheap ones always have those weird hard bits in them, you know? Like, what even is that? Bone? It's gross. My uncle used to buy the cheap ones by the giant pack at Costco. He had this massive chest freezer in his garage that hummed so loud you could hear it from the driveway. One time the power went out for three days after a bad thunderstorm and all the meat in there went bad. The smell was just horrendous. We had to throw out like fifty pounds of hamburger meat. The garbage men wouldn't even take the cans because of the maggots, we had to hose the whole thing down into the street. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, they're definitely working together against me. That's why I have to just keep confronting every single one of them until someone finally cracks and tells the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility, no matter how small, that your family is not hiding your money?\n\nPatient: No, absolutely not. There's zero chance I'm wrong about this. I've told you, it's been over a year of them doing this, and I know what I know. They keep shoving those bank papers in my face, acting like that proves nothing was taken, but I know they're just using all those excuses—the extra bills, the school fundraisers, the trips they're planning—to cover up that they're hiding my money. \n\nSpeaking of trips, my aunt went to Hawaii once and brought back these macadamia nuts covered in chocolate. They were okay, I guess, but I really don't like nuts in my chocolate. It messes up the texture. It's like when people put raisins in cookies and you think it's chocolate chips until you bite into it and it's all squishy and disappointing. I made cookies once for a bake sale when I was still in school, back in seventh grade, I think. I used the slice-and-bake kind with the little pumpkins printed on them for Halloween. They were supposed to be circles, but I sliced them too thin and they burned, so they looked like little black hockey pucks. I tried to scrape the burnt parts off with a butter knife, but it just made crumbs everywhere. My mom got so mad because I didn't wipe down the counters. She's always been crazy about the counters being clean. \n\nBut no, I'm not wrong. I'm going to keep confronting them about my money until they finally admit the truth. There's no possibility I'm making this up.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it difficult to stay on one topic when talking about something important to you?\n\nPatient: What? No, I don't have a hard time staying on topic. I always get right to the point, especially when it's important. Like, I was trying to call the phone company the other day because my bill was wrong, and I told them exactly what the problem was. But they kept me on hold for like forty-five minutes. The hold music was so loud and screechy, it sounded like someone playing a flute underwater. \n\nI actually wanted to play the flute in the school band back in sixth grade. But the teacher made me play the triangle instead because all the flutes were taken. Which is just stupid, you know? Anyone can hit a piece of metal with a little stick. It's not even real music. My brother had a whole drum set in our basement growing up, and he would bang on it all night long. It drove my mom absolutely crazy. She ended up selling it at a yard sale while he was at a friend's house. He was so mad. \n\nYard sales are mostly just people trying to get rid of their garbage anyway. I bought a lamp at one a few years ago, and when I got it home, I realized it didn't even have a plug on the end of the cord. Just bare wires sticking out. So dangerous. You could start a fire with something like that. \n\nBut anyway, no, I don't get off topic. I know exactly what I'm saying and I focus just fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are jumping around a lot when you're talking about different things?\n\nPatient: No, my thoughts don't jump around. I know exactly what I'm thinking about. Why would they jump around? It's not like I'm a frog or something. \n\nThere used to be all these frogs in the creek behind my house when I was little. Big green ones that would croak so loud in the summer you couldn't even sleep with the window open. My neighbor, Mr. Henderson, he hated them. He used to sit on his back porch with a flashlight and just shine it at the water for hours. He was kind of a weird guy, honestly. He always wore those big rubber boots, even when it wasn't raining, and he kept his lawn mowed so short it looked like a golf course. \n\nI tried to play mini golf once, but I couldn't get the ball past the windmill part. The blades kept hitting it away. It's totally rigged, you know? They just want you to keep paying for more tokens. Kind of like the arcade at the mall. I won a giant stuffed bear there once playing skee-ball, but it took up the whole backseat of my car and smelled like old popcorn. I ended up giving it to my cousin's kid, but then the dog chewed the nose off. \n\nBut no, my thoughts are perfectly fine. I just think about what I need to think about. I don't know why you're asking me that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like your mind is racing and you can't control your thoughts?\n\nPatient: No, my mind doesn't race. I'm in total control of what I think about. It's not like a race car or anything. \n\nSpeaking of race cars, my uncle used to take me to the dirt track races on Friday nights when I was a kid. It was so loud you could feel it in your chest, and the dirt would get all up in your teeth. We always got those big funnel cakes with the powdered sugar, but you had to eat them fast before the wind blew the sugar all over your clothes. I wore this black t-shirt once with a picture of a wolf howling at the moon, and it got completely covered in white powder. I looked like a ghost. \n\nI saw a ghost show on TV last week where they go into abandoned hospitals with those little green cameras. They're always yelling about cold spots and hearing voices, but I think it's all fake. Just people trying to get famous. Like those people on the internet who do stupid dances in grocery stores. I was trying to buy cereal the other day—just the regular corn flakes, not the sugary ones because they hurt my teeth—and this girl was blocking the whole aisle doing some weird arm waving thing at her phone. \n\nBut anyway, no, my thoughts don't race. I control my mind just fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to express your thoughts in a logical order?\n\nPatient: Logical order? No, I make perfect sense. Everything I say is in order. It's not like I'm putting my shoes on before my socks. \n\nMy aunt used to knit these really thick wool socks for me every Christmas. They were so itchy, though. I couldn't even wear them for five minutes without my ankles turning bright red. She had this huge basket of yarn in her living room, and her cat—this mean orange tabby named Marmalade—used to sleep right in the middle of it. Marmalade scratched me once when I tried to pet him. Just swiped right at my hand for no reason. \n\nI don't really like cats. Dogs are better. They actually listen to you. My neighbor has a golden retriever that barks at the mailman every single day at exactly 2:00 PM. You can practically set your watch by it. The mailman always looks so annoyed, but he still carries those little dog treats in his pocket. He gave me my mail yesterday and it was all wet because it rained. I hate when the mail gets wet, especially the magazines. The pages stick together and the ink smears. \n\nBut no, my thoughts are totally logical. I don't have any trouble expressing them at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you still believe your family is hiding your money from you?\n\nPatient: Of course they are. I mean, they keep showing me these bank papers, trying to say nothing was taken, but I know what's going on. It's been over a year now, and they're using all these fake bills and fundraisers and talking about planning trips just to cover it up. I have to keep confronting them because they won't tell the truth. \n\nSpeaking of trips, my sister went to Florida last year and she brought back this ridiculous seashell lamp. It was covered in all these little pink and white shells glued together, and the bulb inside was so dim you couldn't even read by it. I tried to plug it in behind my couch, but the cord was too short. \n\nCords are always too short, aren't they? Like the one on my vacuum cleaner. I have to keep switching outlets just to do the living room. It's a real pain. The vacuum is one of those heavy old ones, you know, the kind with the bags you have to change. Last time I changed the bag, dust went everywhere. I was sneezing for an hour. \n\nI don't like sneezing. It always makes my eyes water, and then my mascara runs. I bought this waterproof mascara once, but it was so hard to get off at night I felt like I was scrubbing my eyelashes off. I just gave up and went back to the regular kind. \n\nBut yeah, I came in because they just won't admit what they're doing with my money, and I have to keep pushing until they do.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your mind sometimes jumps from one idea to another without a clear connection?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. My ideas connect perfectly fine. It's not like I'm just jumping around like a frog on lily pads. \n\nLily pads are so weird when you think about it. I went to this pond once behind my old middle school, and it was just covered in them. The water was this murky brown color, and it smelled like old leaves and mud. I tried to catch a frog there once, but it was way too fast. It just bloop, right into the water. \n\nWater is so calming, though. Except when it's freezing cold. My brother pushed me into a pool at a birthday party when I was like, ten, and the water was freezing. I was wearing this pink dress with little white daisies on it, and it got totally ruined. The chlorine bleached the color right out of it. \n\nI hate the smell of chlorine. It always reminds me of that public pool downtown. The locker rooms there always felt so sticky, like they never actually mopped the floors. You had to wear those cheap plastic flip-flops or you felt like you were going to catch something. I bought a pair of blue ones from the dollar store, but the little thong part broke the very first day. \n\nDollar store stuff is always breaking. I bought a can opener there last week and the handle just snapped right off when I tried to open a can of soup. It was chicken noodle, the kind with the really thick noodles. \n\nBut anyway, no, my mind doesn't jump around. I make perfect sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your thoughts are organized and easy to follow when you're explaining something to someone?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, they're organized. The blades kept hitting it away. It's totally rigged, you know? They just want you to keep paying for more tokens. Kind of like the arcade at the mall. I won a giant stuffed bear there once playing skee-ball, but it took up the whole backseat of my car and smelled like old popcorn. I ended up giving it to my cousin's kid, but then the dog chewed the nose off. \n\nBut no, my thoughts are perfectly fine. I just think about what I need to think about. I don't know why you're asking me that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like your mind is racing and you can't control your thoughts?\n\nPatient: No, my mind doesn't race. I'm in total control of what I think about. It's not like a race car or anything. \n\nSpeaking of race cars, my uncle used to take me to the dirt track races on Friday nights when I was a kid. It was so loud you could feel it in your chest, and the dirt would get all up in your teeth. We always got those big funnel cakes with the powdered sugar, but you had to eat them fast before the wind blew the sugar all over your clothes. I wore this black t-shirt once with a picture of a wolf howling at the moon, and it got completely covered in white powder. I looked like a ghost. \n\nI saw a ghost show on TV last week where they go into abandoned hospitals with those little green cameras. They're always yelling about cold spots and hearing voices, but I think it's all fake. Just people trying to get famous. Like those people on the internet who do stupid dances in grocery stores. I was trying to buy cereal the other day—just the regular corn flakes, not the sugary ones because they hurt my teeth—and this girl was blocking the whole aisle doing some weird arm waving thing at her phone. \n\nBut anyway, no, my thoughts don't race. I control my mind just fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to express your thoughts in a logical order?\n\nPatient: Logical order? No, I make perfect sense. Everything I say is in order. It's not like I'm putting my shoes on before my socks. \n\nMy aunt used to knit these really thick wool socks for me every Christmas. They were so itchy, though. I couldn't even wear them for five minutes without my ankles turning bright red. She had this huge basket of yarn in her living room, and her cat—this mean orange tabby named Marmalade—used to sleep right in the middle of it. Marmalade scratched me once when I tried to pet him. Just swiped right at my hand for no reason. \n\nI don't really like cats. Dogs are better. They actually listen to you. My neighbor has a golden retriever that barks at the mailman every single day at exactly 2:00 PM. You can practically set your watch by it. The mailman always looks so annoyed, but he still carries those little dog treats in his pocket. He gave me my mail yesterday and it was all wet because it rained. I hate when the mail gets wet, especially the magazines. The pages stick together and the ink smears. \n\nBut no, my thoughts are totally logical. I don't have any trouble expressing them at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you still believe your family is hiding your money from you?\n\nPatient: Of course they are. I mean, they keep showing me these bank papers, trying to say nothing was taken, but I know what's going on. It's been over a year now, and they're using all these fake bills and fundraisers and talking about planning trips just to cover it up. I have to keep confronting them because they won't tell the truth. \n\nSpeaking of trips, my sister went to Florida last year and she brought back this ridiculous seashell lamp. It was covered in all these little pink and white shells glued together, and the bulb inside was so dim you couldn't even read by it. I tried to plug it in behind my couch, but the cord was too short. \n\nCords are always too short, aren't they? Like the one on my vacuum cleaner. I have to keep switching outlets just to do the living room. It's a real pain. The vacuum is one of those heavy old ones, you know, the kind with the bags you have to change. Last time I changed the bag, dust went everywhere. I was sneezing for an hour. \n\nI don't like sneezing. It always makes my eyes water, and then my mascara runs. I bought this waterproof mascara once, but it was so hard to get off at night I felt like I was scrubbing my eyelashes off. I just gave up and went back to the regular kind. \n\nBut yeah, I came in because they just won't admit what they're doing with my money, and I have to keep pushing until they do.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your mind sometimes jumps from one idea to another without a clear connection?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. My ideas connect perfectly fine. It's not like I'm just jumping around like a frog on lily pads. \n\nLily pads are so weird when you think about it. I went to this pond once behind my old middle school, and it was just covered in them. The water was this murky brown color, and it smelled like old leaves and mud. I tried to catch a frog there once, but it was way too fast. It just bloop, right into the water. \n\nWater is so calming, though. Except when it's freezing cold. My brother pushed me into a pool at a birthday party when I was like, ten, and the water was freezing. I was wearing this pink dress with little white daisies on it, and it got totally ruined. The chlorine bleached the color right out of it. \n\nI hate the smell of chlorine. It always reminds me of that public pool downtown. The locker rooms there always felt so sticky, like they never actually mopped the floors. You had to wear those cheap plastic flip-flops or you felt like you were going to catch something. I bought a pair of blue ones from the dollar store, but the little thong part broke the very first day. \n\nDollar store stuff is always breaking. I bought a can opener there last week and the handle just snapped right off when I tried to open a can of soup. It was chicken noodle, the kind with the really thick noodles. \n\nBut anyway, no, my mind doesn't jump around. I make perfect sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your thoughts are organized and easy to follow when you're explaining something to someone?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, they're organized. I mean, people follow what I'm saying just fine. Like the other day, I was explaining to the cashier at the grocery store how to bag my stuff. You can't put the heavy cans on top of the bread, you know? It squishes it. I hate squished bread. \n\nI tried to make a sandwich last week with this loaf of white bread, and it was all flat and doughy in the middle. I usually use mayonnaise, but I was out, so I had to use mustard. Just regular yellow mustard, not that fancy spicy kind with the little seeds in it. My dad used to put spicy mustard on his hot dogs when we went to the baseball games. The plastic seats there were always so hot in the summer, they'd burn the back of your legs if you wore shorts. I had these denim shorts with the little frayed edges, and I got a sunburn so bad on my knees I couldn't even bend them for days. \n\nAloe vera gel is supposed to help with that, but it's so sticky. It feels like you're rubbing slime all over yourself. Green slime, like from that old TV show where they dumped it on people's heads. I never understood how they got that out of their hair. Hair is so hard to wash when it gets sticky stuff in it. Like gum. I got bubblegum in my hair once when I was little and my mom had to use peanut butter to get it out. We only had crunchy peanut butter, which was a terrible idea because then I just had peanut chunks stuck in my hair instead of gum. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, I explain things perfectly fine. Nobody ever tells me I don't make sense.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

Tangents? No, I don't... I don't really do that. I always stay right on track. Just like a train, you know? 

Actually, there used to be these old railroad tracks right behind my house when I was growing up. We'd put pennies on them and wait for the big freight trains to flatten them out. They got so thin, they looked like little flat copper leaves. I tried to use one of those flattened pennies in a gumball machine once, but it just jammed the whole thing up. The guy at the pizza place was so mad at me. He had this big bushy mustache that twitched when he yelled. It looked exactly like a fuzzy caterpillar sleeping on his upper lip. 

I hate bugs, especially the fuzzy ones. I found a caterpillar on my tomato plant last summer and it ate half the leaves before I even saw it. Tomatoes are just so hard to grow anyway. The birds always peck holes in the good red ones right when they're finally ready to pick. I bought this plastic owl to scare the birds away, but it didn't even work. The wind just blew it over into the neighbor's yard, and their dog chewed its head off. It was a golden retriever, I think. They shed hair everywhere. If you wear black pants around them, you're just ruined for the rest of the day. You have to buy those sticky lint rollers, but the paper always tears wrong when you try to peel off a used sheet. 

But yeah, no, I don't go off on tangents. I always get right to the point.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n13\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F20.0, F60.0\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I came in because my family and my friends keep making a big deal, saying these bank papers prove nothing was taken from me. Which is a joke, because you can print anything on a piece of paper these days. My sister has one of those big printers she got at Walmart when she was trying to start that custom t-shirt business. She made me this pink shirt with a butterfly on it, but the glitter washed right off the first time I wore it. I always tell her you have to turn them inside out to wash them, but she never listens to me. \n\nJust like she doesn't listen when I tell her I know exactly what they're doing. For over a year now, I’ve known they’re all hiding my money. They try to cover it up by talking about all these extra bills they have to pay, or some fundraiser they're doing, or making plans for a big trip... like, who even goes to Florida in the middle of the summer anyway? It's way too humid down there, my hair just frizzes right up and you get sand stuck in your shoes for weeks. But that's exactly how they cover it up, moving it around for trips and things. So I just have to keep confronting them about it. I'm going to keep getting in their faces until they finally tell the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think others are really hiding your money, or is it just a feeling you have?\n\nPatient: It's not just some feeling. I know it for a fact. Like I was just saying, it's been over a year of them doing this. They keep shoving those bank papers at me to prove nothing was taken, but you know how banks are. I went in there once and the teller had these huge fake nails, the kind with the little fake diamonds glued all over them. I don't even get how you type on a keyboard with those things. My cousin got nails exactly like that for her eighth-grade dance and she couldn't even open a can of Dr. Pepper. We had to use a spoon to pop the tab up, and it fizzed up and got all sticky on the kitchen floor. We had to mop twice. I hate mopping, the bucket always smells like old dirty water no matter how much bleach you put in it. \n\nBut yeah, I don't care what they say or what papers they try to show me. I know they're hiding my money behind all those extra bills and trip plans, and I'm gonna keep confronting them until they finally admit it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are connected and make sense when you're talking about your family and the money you think is being hidden?\n\nPatient: Of course they make sense! I don't know why everyone keeps acting like I'm the one who's confused. I mean, it's pretty simple math, right? You put money in, you should have money there. But speaking of math, I was trying to help my neighbor's kid with his homework the other day, and they don't even teach it the normal way anymore. They do this weird box method thing, and I was looking at it like it was written in Chinese. I told him, back when I was in school—well, before I stopped going in eighth grade—we just carried the one. It was so much easier. My math teacher, Mr. Henderson, he always smelled like those peppermint candies. The soft ones, you know? The ones that dissolve in your mouth. He used to keep a whole jar of them on his desk, but he never actually shared them with anybody. Which is pretty rude, if you ask me. If you're gonna have candy out in plain sight, you should offer it. Just like if you have someone's money, you shouldn't be spending it on trips to Florida. So yeah, my thoughts make perfect sense. They're just the ones trying to confuse everything.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your family is working together against you on purpose?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, like I've been telling you, they are absolutely all in on it together. My family, my friends, all of them. It's totally on purpose. They think they're so smart, passing around those same stupid bank papers trying to tell me nothing was taken, but I know for a fact they're coordinating it. They're hiding my money and using all those excuses I told you about—the extra bills, the fundraisers, the trips—just to cover their tracks. \n\nI actually saw my sister and my cousin whispering about it at a family BBQ last month. We were having hot dogs, and honestly, I don't even like hot dogs unless they're the all-beef ones. The cheap ones always have those weird hard bits in them, you know? Like, what even is that? Bone? It's gross. My uncle used to buy the cheap ones by the giant pack at Costco. He had this massive chest freezer in his garage that hummed so loud you could hear it from the driveway. One time the power went out for three days after a bad thunderstorm and all the meat in there went bad. The smell was just horrendous. We had to throw out like fifty pounds of hamburger meat. The garbage men wouldn't even take the cans because of the maggots, we had to hose the whole thing down into the street. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, they're definitely working together against me. That's why I have to just keep confronting every single one of them until someone finally cracks and tells the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility, no matter how small, that your family is not hiding your money?\n\nPatient: No, absolutely not. There's zero chance I'm wrong about this. I've told you, it's been over a year of them doing this, and I know what I know. They keep shoving those bank papers in my face, acting like that proves nothing was taken, but I know they're just using all those excuses—the extra bills, the school fundraisers, the trips they're planning—to cover up that they're hiding my money. \n\nSpeaking of trips, my aunt went to Hawaii once and brought back these macadamia nuts covered in chocolate. They were okay, I guess, but I really don't like nuts in my chocolate. It messes up the texture. It's like when people put raisins in cookies and you think it's chocolate chips until you bite into it and it's all squishy and disappointing. I made cookies once for a bake sale when I was still in school, back in seventh grade, I think. I used the slice-and-bake kind with the little pumpkins printed on them for Halloween. They were supposed to be circles, but I sliced them too thin and they burned, so they looked like little black hockey pucks. I tried to scrape the burnt parts off with a butter knife, but it just made crumbs everywhere. My mom got so mad because I didn't wipe down the counters. She's always been crazy about the counters being clean. \n\nBut no, I'm not wrong. I'm going to keep confronting them about my money until they finally admit the truth. There's no possibility I'm making this up.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it difficult to stay on one topic when talking about something important to you?\n\nPatient: What? No, I don't have a hard time staying on topic. I always get right to the point, especially when it's important. Like, I was trying to call the phone company the other day because my bill was wrong, and I told them exactly what the problem was. But they kept me on hold for like forty-five minutes. The hold music was so loud and screechy, it sounded like someone playing a flute underwater. \n\nI actually wanted to play the flute in the school band back in sixth grade. But the teacher made me play the triangle instead because all the flutes were taken. Which is just stupid, you know? Anyone can hit a piece of metal with a little stick. It's not even real music. My brother had a whole drum set in our basement growing up, and he would bang on it all night long. It drove my mom absolutely crazy. She ended up selling it at a yard sale while he was at a friend's house. He was so mad. \n\nYard sales are mostly just people trying to get rid of their garbage anyway. I bought a lamp at one a few years ago, and when I got it home, I realized it didn't even have a plug on the end of the cord. Just bare wires sticking out. So dangerous. You could start a fire with something like that. \n\nBut anyway, no, I don't get off topic. I know exactly what I'm saying and I focus just fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are jumping around a lot when you're talking about different things?\n\nPatient: No, my thoughts don't jump around. I know exactly what I'm thinking about. Why would they jump around? It's not like I'm a frog or something. \n\nThere used to be all these frogs in the creek behind my house when I was little. Big green ones that would croak so loud in the summer you couldn't even sleep with the window open. My neighbor, Mr. Henderson, he hated them. He used to sit on his back porch with a flashlight and just shine it at the water for hours. He was kind of a weird guy, honestly. He always wore those big rubber boots, even when it wasn't raining, and he kept his lawn mowed so short it looked like a golf course. \n\nI tried to play mini golf once, but I couldn't get the ball past the windmill part. The blades kept hitting it away. It's totally rigged, you know? They just want you to keep paying for more tokens. Kind of like the arcade at the mall. I won a giant stuffed bear there once playing skee-ball, but it took up the whole backseat of my car and smelled like old popcorn. I ended up giving it to my cousin's kid, but then the dog chewed the nose off. \n\nBut no, my thoughts are perfectly fine. I just think about what I need to think about. I don't know why you're asking me that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like your mind is racing and you can't control your thoughts?\n\nPatient: No, my mind doesn't race. I'm in total control of what I think about. It's not like a race car or anything. \n\nSpeaking of race cars, my uncle used to take me to the dirt track races on Friday nights when I was a kid. It was so loud you could feel it in your chest, and the dirt would get all up in your teeth. We always got those big funnel cakes with the powdered sugar, but you had to eat them fast before the wind blew the sugar all over your clothes. I wore this black t-shirt once with a picture of a wolf howling at the moon, and it got completely covered in white powder. I looked like a ghost. \n\nI saw a ghost show on TV last week where they go into abandoned hospitals with those little green cameras. They're always yelling about cold spots and hearing voices, but I think it's all fake. Just people trying to get famous. Like those people on the internet who do stupid dances in grocery stores. I was trying to buy cereal the other day—just the regular corn flakes, not the sugary ones because they hurt my teeth—and this girl was blocking the whole aisle doing some weird arm waving thing at her phone. \n\nBut anyway, no, my thoughts don't race. I control my mind just fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to express your thoughts in a logical order?\n\nPatient: Logical order? No, I make perfect sense. Everything I say is in order. It's not like I'm putting my shoes on before my socks. \n\nMy aunt used to knit these really thick wool socks for me every Christmas. They were so itchy, though. I couldn't even wear them for five minutes without my ankles turning bright red. She had this huge basket of yarn in her living room, and her cat—this mean orange tabby named Marmalade—used to sleep right in the middle of it. Marmalade scratched me once when I tried to pet him. Just swiped right at my hand for no reason. \n\nI don't really like cats. Dogs are better. They actually listen to you. My neighbor has a golden retriever that barks at the mailman every single day at exactly 2:00 PM. You can practically set your watch by it. The mailman always looks so annoyed, but he still carries those little dog treats in his pocket. He gave me my mail yesterday and it was all wet because it rained. I hate when the mail gets wet, especially the magazines. The pages stick together and the ink smears. \n\nBut no, my thoughts are totally logical. I don't have any trouble expressing them at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you still believe your family is hiding your money from you?\n\nPatient: Of course they are. I mean, they keep showing me these bank papers, trying to say nothing was taken, but I know what's going on. It's been over a year now, and they're using all these fake bills and fundraisers and talking about planning trips just to cover it up. I have to keep confronting them because they won't tell the truth. \n\nSpeaking of trips, my sister went to Florida last year and she brought back this ridiculous seashell lamp. It was covered in all these little pink and white shells glued together, and the bulb inside was so dim you couldn't even read by it. I tried to plug it in behind my couch, but the cord was too short. \n\nCords are always too short, aren't they? Like the one on my vacuum cleaner. I have to keep switching outlets just to do the living room. It's a real pain. The vacuum is one of those heavy old ones, you know, the kind with the bags you have to change. Last time I changed the bag, dust went everywhere. I was sneezing for an hour. \n\nI don't like sneezing. It always makes my eyes water, and then my mascara runs. I bought this waterproof mascara once, but it was so hard to get off at night I felt like I was scrubbing my eyelashes off. I just gave up and went back to the regular kind. \n\nBut yeah, I came in because they just won't admit what they're doing with my money, and I have to keep pushing until they do.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your mind sometimes jumps from one idea to another without a clear connection?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. My ideas connect perfectly fine. It's not like I'm just jumping around like a frog on lily pads. \n\nLily pads are so weird when you think about it. I went to this pond once behind my old middle school, and it was just covered in them. The water was this murky brown color, and it smelled like old leaves and mud. I tried to catch a frog there once, but it was way too fast. It just bloop, right into the water. \n\nWater is so calming, though. Except when it's freezing cold. My brother pushed me into a pool at a birthday party when I was like, ten, and the water was freezing. I was wearing this pink dress with little white daisies on it, and it got totally ruined. The chlorine bleached the color right out of it. \n\nI hate the smell of chlorine. It always reminds me of that public pool downtown. The locker rooms there always felt so sticky, like they never actually mopped the floors. You had to wear those cheap plastic flip-flops or you felt like you were going to catch something. I bought a pair of blue ones from the dollar store, but the little thong part broke the very first day. \n\nDollar store stuff is always breaking. I bought a can opener there last week and the handle just snapped right off when I tried to open a can of soup. It was chicken noodle, the kind with the really thick noodles. \n\nBut anyway, no, my mind doesn't jump around. I make perfect sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your thoughts are organized and easy to follow when you're explaining something to someone?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, they're organized. I mean, people follow what I'm saying just fine. Like the other day, I was explaining to the cashier at the grocery store how to bag my stuff. You can't put the heavy cans on top of the bread, you know? It squishes it. I hate squished bread. \n\nI tried to make a sandwich last week with this loaf of white bread, and it was all flat and doughy in the middle. I usually use mayonnaise, but I was out, so I had to use mustard. Just regular yellow mustard, not that fancy spicy kind with the little seeds in it. My dad used to put spicy mustard on his hot dogs when we went to the baseball games. The plastic seats there were always so hot in the summer, they'd burn the back of your legs if you wore shorts. I had these denim shorts with the little frayed edges, and I got a sunburn so bad on my knees I couldn't even bend them for days. \n\nAloe vera gel is supposed to help with that, but it's so sticky. It feels like you're rubbing slime all over yourself. Green slime, like from that old TV show where they dumped it on people's heads. I never understood how they got that out of their hair. Hair is so hard to wash when it gets sticky stuff in it. Like gum. I got bubblegum in my hair once when I was little and my mom had to use peanut butter to get it out. We only had crunchy peanut butter, which was a terrible idea because then I just had peanut chunks stuck in my hair instead of gum. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, I explain things perfectly fine. Nobody ever tells me I don't make sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you often go off on tangents when trying to explain something?\n\nPatient: Tangents? No, I don't... I don't really do that. I always stay right on track. Just like a train, you know? \n\nActually, there used to be these old railroad tracks right behind my house when I was growing up. We'd put pennies on them and wait for the big freight trains to flatten them out. They got so thin, they looked like little flat copper leaves. I tried to use one of those flattened pennies in a gumball machine once, but it just jammed the whole thing up. The guy at the pizza place was so mad at me. He had this big bushy mustache that twitched when he yelled. It looked exactly like a fuzzy caterpillar sleeping on his upper lip. \n\nI hate bugs, especially the fuzzy ones. I found a caterpillar on my tomato plant last summer and it ate half the leaves before I even saw it. Tomatoes are just so hard to grow anyway. The birds always peck holes in the good red ones right when they're finally ready to pick. I bought this plastic owl to scare the birds away, but it didn't even work. The wind just blew it over into the neighbor's yard, and their dog chewed its head off. It was a golden retriever, I think. They shed hair everywhere. If you wear black pants around them, you're just ruined for the rest of the day. You have to buy those sticky lint rollers, but the paper always tears wrong when you try to peel off a used sheet. \n\nBut yeah, no, I don't go off on tangents. I always get right to the point.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Disconnected? No, my thoughts aren't disconnected. They're all strung together just fine, like a pearl necklace. 

My grandmother had this beautiful pearl necklace she kept in a little velvet box on her dresser. The velvet was this really deep, rich blue color, almost like the sky right after the sun goes down but before it gets completely dark. I used to love looking out the window at that time of day when I lived in my old apartment. The streetlights would just start flickering on, and they had that weird orange glow. It always made the shadows look super long and creepy on the sidewalk. 

I tripped on a crack in the sidewalk once when I was walking to the corner store to buy milk. I scraped my knee so bad it bled through my jeans. They were my favorite jeans, too, the ones with the little rhinestones on the back pockets. I tried to wash the blood out with cold water and soap, but it just left this ugly brown stain. Stains are impossible to get out sometimes, especially coffee. I spilled coffee on a library book once, and they made me pay for the whole thing. It was a book about gardening, which is funny because I don't even have a garden anymore. I just have a couple of sad-looking houseplants that I always forget to water. 

So, no, my thoughts are perfectly connected. I don't know why you'd think otherwise.

They were okay, I guess, but I really don't like nuts in my chocolate. It messes up the texture. It's like when people put raisins in cookies and you think it's chocolate chips until you bite into it and it's all squishy and disappointing. I made cookies once for a bake sale when I was still in school, back in seventh grade, I think. I used the slice-and-bake kind with the little pumpkins printed on them for Halloween. They were supposed to be circles, but I sliced them too thin and they burned, so they looked like little black hockey pucks. No, I don't have a hard time staying on topic. I always get right to the point, especially when it's important. The hold music was so loud and screechy, it sounded like someone playing a flute underwater. \n\nI actually wanted to play the flute in the school band back in sixth grade. But the teacher made me play the triangle instead because all the flutes were taken. Which is just stupid, you know? Anyone can hit a piece of metal with a little stick. It's not even real music. My brother had a whole drum set in our basement growing up, and he would bang on it all night long. It drove my mom absolutely crazy. She ended up selling it at a yard sale while he was at a friend's house. He was so mad. \n\nYard sales are mostly just people trying to get rid of their garbage anyway. I know exactly what I'm thinking about. Why would they jump around? It's not like I'm a frog or something. \n\nThere used to be all these frogs in the creek behind my house when I was little. He used to sit on his back porch with a flashlight and just shine it at the water for hours. He was kind of a weird guy, honestly. I'm in total control of what I think about. It's not like a race car or anything. \n\nSpeaking of race cars, my uncle used to take me to the dirt track races on Friday nights when I was a kid. It was so loud you could feel it in your chest, and the dirt would get all up in your teeth. We always got those big funnel cakes with the powdered sugar, but you had to eat them fast before the wind blew the sugar all over your clothes. I wore this black t-shirt once with a picture of a wolf howling at the moon, and it got completely covered in white powder. I looked like a ghost. \n\nI saw a ghost show on TV last week where they go into abandoned hospitals with those little green cameras. I was trying to buy cereal the other day—just the regular corn flakes, not the sugary ones because they hurt my teeth—and this girl was blocking the whole aisle doing some weird arm waving thing at her phone. \n\nBut anyway, no, my thoughts don't race. The mailman always looks so annoyed, but he still carries those little dog treats in his pocket. He gave me my mail yesterday and it was all wet because it rained. I hate when the mail gets wet, especially the magazines. The pages stick together and the ink smears. \n\nBut no, my thoughts are totally logical. I don't have any trouble expressing them at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you still believe your family is hiding your money from you?\n\nPatient: Of course they are. I mean, they keep showing me these bank papers, trying to say nothing was taken, but I know what's going on. It's been over a year now, and they're using all these fake bills and fundraisers and talking about planning trips just to cover it up. I have to keep confronting them because they won't tell the truth. \n\nSpeaking of trips, my sister went to Florida last year and she brought back this ridiculous seashell lamp. It was covered in all these little pink and white shells glued together, and the bulb inside was so dim you couldn't even read by it. I tried to plug it in behind my couch, but the cord was too short. \n\nCords are always too short, aren't they? Like the one on my vacuum cleaner. I have to keep switching outlets just to do the living room. It's a real pain. The vacuum is one of those heavy old ones, you know, the kind with the bags you have to change. Last time I changed the bag, dust went everywhere. I was sneezing for an hour. \n\nI don't like sneezing. It always makes my eyes water, and then my mascara runs. I bought this waterproof mascara once, but it was so hard to get off at night I felt like I was scrubbing my eyelashes off. I just gave up and went back to the regular kind. \n\nBut yeah, I came in because they just won't admit what they're doing with my money, and I have to keep pushing until they do.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your mind sometimes jumps from one idea to another without a clear connection?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. My ideas connect perfectly fine. It's not like I'm just jumping around like a frog on lily pads. \n\nLily pads are so weird when you think about it. I went to this pond once behind my old middle school, and it was just covered in them. The water was this murky brown color, and it smelled like old leaves and mud. I tried to catch a frog there once, but it was way too fast. It just bloop, right into the water. \n\nWater is so calming, though. Except when it's freezing cold. My brother pushed me into a pool at a birthday party when I was like, ten, and the water was freezing. I was wearing this pink dress with little white daisies on it, and it got totally ruined. The chlorine bleached the color right out of it. \n\nI hate the smell of chlorine. It always reminds me of that public pool downtown. The locker rooms there always felt so sticky, like they never actually mopped the floors. You had to wear those cheap plastic flip-flops or you felt like you were going to catch something. I bought a pair of blue ones from the dollar store, but the little thong part broke the very first day. \n\nDollar store stuff is always breaking. I bought a can opener there last week and the handle just snapped right off when I tried to open a can of soup. It was chicken noodle, the kind with the really thick noodles. \n\nBut anyway, no, my mind doesn't jump around. I make perfect sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your thoughts are organized and easy to follow when you're explaining something to someone?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, they're organized. I mean, people follow what I'm saying just fine. Like the other day, I was explaining to the cashier at the grocery store how to bag my stuff. My dad used to put spicy mustard on his hot dogs when we went to the baseball games. The plastic seats there were always so hot in the summer, they'd burn the back of your legs if you wore shorts. I had these denim shorts with the little frayed edges, and I got a sunburn so bad on my knees I couldn't even bend them for days. \n\nAloe vera gel is supposed to help with that, but it's so sticky. It feels like you're rubbing slime all over yourself. Green slime, like from that old TV show where they dumped it on people's heads. I never understood how they got that out of their hair. Hair is so hard to wash when it gets sticky stuff in it. Like gum. I got bubblegum in my hair once when I was little and my mom had to use peanut butter to get it out. We only had crunchy peanut butter, which was a terrible idea because then I just had peanut chunks stuck in my hair instead of gum. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, I explain things perfectly fine. The birds always peck holes in the good red ones right when they're finally ready to pick. I bought this plastic owl to scare the birds away, but it didn't even work. The wind just blew it over into the neighbor's yard, and their dog chewed its head off. It was a golden retriever, I think. They shed hair everywhere. If you wear black pants around them, you're just ruined for the rest of the day. You have to buy those sticky lint rollers, but the paper always tears wrong when you try to peel off a used sheet. \n\nBut yeah, no, I don't go off on tangents. I always get right to the point.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are often disconnected from each other?\n\nPatient: Disconnected? No, my thoughts aren't disconnected. I don't know why you'd think otherwise.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. You can't put the heavy cans on top of the bread, you know? It squishes it. I hate squished bread. \n\nI tried to make a sandwich last week with this loaf of white bread, and it was all flat and doughy in the middle. I usually use mayonnaise, but I was out, so I had to use mustard. Just regular yellow mustard, not that fancy spicy kind with the little seeds in it. My dad used to put spicy mustard on his hot dogs when we went to the baseball games. The plastic seats there were always so hot in the summer, they'd burn the back of your legs if you wore shorts. I had these denim shorts with the little frayed edges, and I got a sunburn so bad on my knees I couldn't even bend them for days. \n\nAloe vera gel is supposed to help with that, but it's so sticky. It feels like you're rubbing slime all over yourself. Green slime, like from that old TV show where they dumped it on people's heads. I never understood how they got that out of their hair. Hair is so hard to wash when it gets sticky stuff in it. Like gum. I got bubblegum in my hair once when I was little and my mom had to use peanut butter to get it out. We only had crunchy peanut butter, which was a terrible idea because then I just had peanut chunks stuck in my hair instead of gum. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, I explain things perfectly fine. Nobody ever tells me I don't make sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you often go off on tangents when trying to explain something?\n\nPatient: Tangents? No, I don't... I don't really do that. I always stay right on track. Just like a train, you know? \n\nActually, there used to be these old railroad tracks right behind my house when I was growing up. We'd put pennies on them and wait for the big freight trains to flatten them out. They got so thin, they looked like little flat copper leaves. I tried to use one of those flattened pennies in a gumball machine once, but it just jammed the whole thing up. The guy at the pizza place was so mad at me. He had this big bushy mustache that twitched when he yelled. It looked exactly like a fuzzy caterpillar sleeping on his upper lip. \n\nI hate bugs, especially the fuzzy ones. I found a caterpillar on my tomato plant last summer and it ate half the leaves before I even saw it. Tomatoes are just so hard to grow anyway. The birds always peck holes in the good red ones right when they're finally ready to pick. I bought this plastic owl to scare the birds away, but it didn't even work. The wind just blew it over into the neighbor's yard, and their dog chewed its head off. It was a golden retriever, I think. They shed hair everywhere. If you wear black pants around them, you're just ruined for the rest of the day. You have to buy those sticky lint rollers, but the paper always tears wrong when you try to peel off a used sheet. \n\nBut yeah, no, I don't go off on tangents. I always get right to the point.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are often disconnected from each other?\n\nPatient: Disconnected? No, my thoughts aren't disconnected. They're all strung together just fine, like a pearl necklace. \n\nMy grandmother had this beautiful pearl necklace she kept in a little velvet box on her dresser. The velvet was this really deep, rich blue color, almost like the sky right after the sun goes down but before it gets completely dark. I used to love looking out the window at that time of day when I lived in my old apartment. The streetlights would just start flickering on, and they had that weird orange glow. It always made the shadows look super long and creepy on the sidewalk. \n\nI tripped on a crack in the sidewalk once when I was walking to the corner store to buy milk. I scraped my knee so bad it bled through my jeans. They were my favorite jeans, too, the ones with the little rhinestones on the back pockets. I tried to wash the blood out with cold water and soap, but it just left this ugly brown stain. Stains are impossible to get out sometimes, especially coffee. I spilled coffee on a library book once, and they made me pay for the whole thing. It was a book about gardening, which is funny because I don't even have a garden anymore. I just have a couple of sad-looking houseplants that I always forget to water. \n\nSo, no, my thoughts are perfectly connected. I don't know why you'd think otherwise.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you still believe that your family is hiding money from you?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes, of course they are! I've known it for over a year now. They keep showing me these bank papers, trying to prove nothing was taken, but I know they're hiding my money. They use all these fake bills, and these so-called fundraisers, and plans for trips to cover it all up. I just have to keep confronting them until they finally tell the truth. 

It's just like when my cousin said she was raising money for the animal shelter. She had these little flyers printed out on this bright pink paper. It was the exact same color as the frosting on the birthday cake I had when I turned ten. My mom made it from scratch, and it was strawberry flavored. I remember because I ate so much of it I got a terrible stomach ache and had to lay on the couch all afternoon watching cartoons. 

We only had like three channels back then, and the reception was always fuzzy unless you stood right next to the TV and held the antenna just right. My brother used to make me stand there for an hour while he watched his wrestling shows. He loved wrestling. He had all these action figures he'd smash together on the living room rug. It was a shag rug, kind of a mustard yellow color, and it was impossible to vacuum. You'd always find little plastic weapons or pennies buried deep in the fibers. 

Pennies are so useless now anyway. You can't even buy a piece of candy for a penny anymore. I remember when you could go to the corner store and get those little Swedish Fish for a penny a piece. They were so chewy they'd get stuck in your teeth for hours. You'd have to use a toothpick to dig them out, but if you pushed too hard, your gums would bleed. 

But yeah, I know they're hiding it, and I won't stop until they admit it.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I came in because my family and my friends keep making a big deal, saying these bank papers prove nothing was taken from me. She made me this pink shirt with a butterfly on it, but the glitter washed right off the first time I wore it. I always tell her you have to turn them inside out to wash them, but she never listens to me. \n\nJust like she doesn't listen when I tell her I know exactly what they're doing. For over a year now, I’ve known they’re all hiding my money. They try to cover it up by talking about all these extra bills they have to pay, or some fundraiser they're doing, or making plans for a big trip... like, who even goes to Florida in the middle of the summer anyway? It's way too humid down there, my hair just frizzes right up and you get sand stuck in your shoes for weeks. But that's exactly how they cover it up, moving it around for trips and things. So I just have to keep confronting them about it. I'm going to keep getting in their faces until they finally tell the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think others are really hiding your money, or is it just a feeling you have?\n\nPatient: It's not just some feeling. I know it for a fact. Like I was just saying, it's been over a year of them doing this. They keep shoving those bank papers at me to prove nothing was taken, but you know how banks are. I went in there once and the teller had these huge fake nails, the kind with the little fake diamonds glued all over them. I don't even get how you type on a keyboard with those things. I don't know why everyone keeps acting like I'm the one who's confused. I mean, it's pretty simple math, right? You put money in, you should have money there. But speaking of math, I was trying to help my neighbor's kid with his homework the other day, and they don't even teach it the normal way anymore. They do this weird box method thing, and I was looking at it like it was written in Chinese. I told him, back when I was in school—well, before I stopped going in eighth grade—we just carried the one. It was so much easier. My math teacher, Mr. Henderson, he always smelled like those peppermint candies. The soft ones, you know? The ones that dissolve in your mouth. He used to keep a whole jar of them on his desk, but he never actually shared them with anybody. Which is pretty rude, if you ask me. If you're gonna have candy out in plain sight, you should offer it. Just like if you have someone's money, you shouldn't be spending it on trips to Florida. So yeah, my thoughts make perfect sense. They're just the ones trying to confuse everything.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your family is working together against you on purpose?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, like I've been telling you, they are absolutely all in on it together. My family, my friends, all of them. It's totally on purpose. They think they're so smart, passing around those same stupid bank papers trying to tell me nothing was taken, but I know for a fact they're coordinating it. They're hiding my money and using all those excuses I told you about—the extra bills, the fundraisers, the trips—just to cover their tracks. \n\nI actually saw my sister and my cousin whispering about it at a family BBQ last month. We were having hot dogs, and honestly, I don't even like hot dogs unless they're the all-beef ones. The cheap ones always have those weird hard bits in them, you know? Like, what even is that? Bone? It's gross. My uncle used to buy the cheap ones by the giant pack at Costco. He had this massive chest freezer in his garage that hummed so loud you could hear it from the driveway. One time the power went out for three days after a bad thunderstorm and all the meat in there went bad. The smell was just horrendous. We had to throw out like fifty pounds of hamburger meat. The garbage men wouldn't even take the cans because of the maggots, we had to hose the whole thing down into the street. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, they're definitely working together against me. That's why I have to just keep confronting every single one of them until someone finally cracks and tells the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility, no matter how small, that your family is not hiding your money?\n\nPatient: No, absolutely not. There's zero chance I'm wrong about this. I've told you, it's been over a year of them doing this, and I know what I know. They keep shoving those bank papers in my face, acting like that proves nothing was taken, but I know they're just using all those excuses—the extra bills, the school fundraisers, the trips they're planning—to cover up that they're hiding my money. \n\nSpeaking of trips, my aunt went to Hawaii once and brought back these macadamia nuts covered in chocolate. They were okay, I guess, but I really don't like nuts in my chocolate. It messes up the texture. It's like when people put raisins in cookies and you think it's chocolate chips until you bite into it and it's all squishy and disappointing. I made cookies once for a bake sale when I was still in school, back in seventh grade, I think. I used the slice-and-bake kind with the little pumpkins printed on them for Halloween. They were supposed to be circles, but I sliced them too thin and they burned, so they looked like little black hockey pucks. I tried to scrape the burnt parts off with a butter knife, but it just made crumbs everywhere. My mom got so mad because I didn't wipe down the counters. She's always been crazy about the counters being clean. \n\nBut no, I'm not wrong. I'm going to keep confronting them about my money until they finally admit the truth. There's no possibility I'm making this up.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it difficult to stay on one topic when talking about something important to you?\n\nPatient: What? No, I don't have a hard time staying on topic. I always get right to the point, especially when it's important. Like, I was trying to call the phone company the other day because my bill was wrong, and I told them exactly what the problem was. But they kept me on hold for like forty-five minutes. The hold music was so loud and screechy, it sounded like someone playing a flute underwater. \n\nI actually wanted to play the flute in the school band back in sixth grade. But the teacher made me play the triangle instead because all the flutes were taken. Which is just stupid, you know? Anyone can hit a piece of metal with a little stick. It's not even real music. My brother had a whole drum set in our basement growing up, and he would bang on it all night long. It drove my mom absolutely crazy. She ended up selling it at a yard sale while he was at a friend's house. He was so mad. \n\nYard sales are mostly just people trying to get rid of their garbage anyway. I bought a lamp at one a few years ago, and when I got it home, I realized it didn't even have a plug on the end of the cord. Just bare wires sticking out. So dangerous. You could start a fire with something like that. \n\nBut anyway, no, I don't get off topic. I know exactly what I'm saying and I focus just fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are jumping around a lot when you're talking about different things?\n\nPatient: No, my thoughts don't jump around. I know exactly what I'm thinking about. Why would they jump around? It's not like I'm a frog or something. \n\nThere used to be all these frogs in the creek behind my house when I was little. Big green ones that would croak so loud in the summer you couldn't even sleep with the window open. My neighbor, Mr. Henderson, he hated them. He used to sit on his back porch with a flashlight and just shine it at the water for hours. He was kind of a weird guy, honestly. He always wore those big rubber boots, even when it wasn't raining, and he kept his lawn mowed so short it looked like a golf course. \n\nI tried to play mini golf once, but I couldn't get the ball past the windmill part. The blades kept hitting it away. It's totally rigged, you know? They just want you to keep paying for more tokens. Kind of like the arcade at the mall. I won a giant stuffed bear there once playing skee-ball, but it took up the whole backseat of my car and smelled like old popcorn. I ended up giving it to my cousin's kid, but then the dog chewed the nose off. \n\nBut no, my thoughts are perfectly fine. I just think about what I need to think about. I don't know why you're asking me that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like your mind is racing and you can't control your thoughts?\n\nPatient: No, my mind doesn't race. I'm in total control of what I think about. It's not like a race car or anything. \n\nSpeaking of race cars, my uncle used to take me to the dirt track races on Friday nights when I was a kid. It was so loud you could feel it in your chest, and the dirt would get all up in your teeth. We always got those big funnel cakes with the powdered sugar, but you had to eat them fast before the wind blew the sugar all over your clothes. I wore this black t-shirt once with a picture of a wolf howling at the moon, and it got completely covered in white powder. I looked like a ghost. \n\nI saw a ghost show on TV last week where they go into abandoned hospitals with those little green cameras. They're always yelling about cold spots and hearing voices, but I think it's all fake. Just people trying to get famous. Like those people on the internet who do stupid dances in grocery stores. I was trying to buy cereal the other day—just the regular corn flakes, not the sugary ones because they hurt my teeth—and this girl was blocking the whole aisle doing some weird arm waving thing at her phone. \n\nBut anyway, no, my thoughts don't race. I control my mind just fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to express your thoughts in a logical order?\n\nPatient: Logical order? No, I make perfect sense. Everything I say is in order. It's not like I'm putting my shoes on before my socks. \n\nMy aunt used to knit these really thick wool socks for me every Christmas. They were so itchy, though. I couldn't even wear them for five minutes without my ankles turning bright red. She had this huge basket of yarn in her living room, and her cat—this mean orange tabby named Marmalade—used to sleep right in the middle of it. Marmalade scratched me once when I tried to pet him. Just swiped right at my hand for no reason. \n\nI don't really like cats. Dogs are better. They actually listen to you. My neighbor has a golden retriever that barks at the mailman every single day at exactly 2:00 PM. You can practically set your watch by it. The mailman always looks so annoyed, but he still carries those little dog treats in his pocket. He gave me my mail yesterday and it was all wet because it rained. I hate when the mail gets wet, especially the magazines. The pages stick together and the ink smears. \n\nBut no, my thoughts are totally logical. I don't have any trouble expressing them at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you still believe your family is hiding your money from you?\n\nPatient: Of course they are. I mean, they keep showing me these bank papers, trying to say nothing was taken, but I know what's going on. It's been over a year now, and they're using all these fake bills and fundraisers and talking about planning trips just to cover it up. I have to keep confronting them because they won't tell the truth. \n\nSpeaking of trips, my sister went to Florida last year and she brought back this ridiculous seashell lamp. It was covered in all these little pink and white shells glued together, and the bulb inside was so dim you couldn't even read by it. I tried to plug it in behind my couch, but the cord was too short. \n\nCords are always too short, aren't they? Like the one on my vacuum cleaner. I have to keep switching outlets just to do the living room. It's a real pain. The vacuum is one of those heavy old ones, you know, the kind with the bags you have to change. Last time I changed the bag, dust went everywhere. I was sneezing for an hour. \n\nI don't like sneezing. It always makes my eyes water, and then my mascara runs. I bought this waterproof mascara once, but it was so hard to get off at night I felt like I was scrubbing my eyelashes off. I just gave up and went back to the regular kind. \n\nBut yeah, I came in because they just won't admit what they're doing with my money, and I have to keep pushing until they do.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your mind sometimes jumps from one idea to another without a clear connection?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. My ideas connect perfectly fine. It's not like I'm just jumping around like a frog on lily pads. \n\nLily pads are so weird when you think about it. I went to this pond once behind my old middle school, and it was just covered in them. The water was this murky brown color, and it smelled like old leaves and mud. I tried to catch a frog there once, but it was way too fast. It just bloop, right into the water. \n\nWater is so calming, though. Except when it's freezing cold. My brother pushed me into a pool at a birthday party when I was like, ten, and the water was freezing. I was wearing this pink dress with little white daisies on it, and it got totally ruined. The chlorine bleached the color right out of it. \n\nI hate the smell of chlorine. It always reminds me of that public pool downtown. The locker rooms there always felt so sticky, like they never actually mopped the floors. You had to wear those cheap plastic flip-flops or you felt like you were going to catch something. I bought a pair of blue ones from the dollar store, but the little thong part broke the very first day. \n\nDollar store stuff is always breaking. I bought a can opener there last week and the handle just snapped right off when I tried to open a can of soup. It was chicken noodle, the kind with the really thick noodles. \n\nBut anyway, no, my mind doesn't jump around. I make perfect sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your thoughts are organized and easy to follow when you're explaining something to someone?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, they're organized. I mean, people follow what I'm saying just fine. Like the other day, I was explaining to the cashier at the grocery store how to bag my stuff. You can't put the heavy cans on top of the bread, you know? It squishes it. I hate squished bread. \n\nI tried to make a sandwich last week with this loaf of white bread, and it was all flat and doughy in the middle. I usually use mayonnaise, but I was out, so I had to use mustard. Just regular yellow mustard, not that fancy spicy kind with the little seeds in it. My dad used to put spicy mustard on his hot dogs when we went to the baseball games. The plastic seats there were always so hot in the summer, they'd burn the back of your legs if you wore shorts. I had these denim shorts with the little frayed edges, and I got a sunburn so bad on my knees I couldn't even bend them for days. \n\nAloe vera gel is supposed to help with that, but it's so sticky. It feels like you're rubbing slime all over yourself. Green slime, like from that old TV show where they dumped it on people's heads. I never understood how they got that out of their hair. Hair is so hard to wash when it gets sticky stuff in it. Like gum. I got bubblegum in my hair once when I was little and my mom had to use peanut butter to get it out. We only had crunchy peanut butter, which was a terrible idea because then I just had peanut chunks stuck in my hair instead of gum. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, I explain things perfectly fine. Nobody ever tells me I don't make sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you often go off on tangents when trying to explain something?\n\nPatient: Tangents? No, I don't... I don't really do that. I always stay right on track. Just like a train, you know? \n\nActually, there used to be these old railroad tracks right behind my house when I was growing up. We'd put pennies on them and wait for the big freight trains to flatten them out. They got so thin, they looked like little flat copper leaves. I tried to use one of those flattened pennies in a gumball machine once, but it just jammed the whole thing up. The guy at the pizza place was so mad at me. He had this big bushy mustache that twitched when he yelled. It looked exactly like a fuzzy caterpillar sleeping on his upper lip. \n\nI hate bugs, especially the fuzzy ones. I found a caterpillar on my tomato plant last summer and it ate half the leaves before I even saw it. Tomatoes are just so hard to grow anyway. The birds always peck holes in the good red ones right when they're finally ready to pick. I bought this plastic owl to scare the birds away, but it didn't even work. The wind just blew it over into the neighbor's yard, and their dog chewed its head off. It was a golden retriever, I think. They shed hair everywhere. If you wear black pants around them, you're just ruined for the rest of the day. You have to buy those sticky lint rollers, but the paper always tears wrong when you try to peel off a used sheet. \n\nBut yeah, no, I don't go off on tangents. I always get right to the point.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are often disconnected from each other?\n\nPatient: Disconnected? No, my thoughts aren't disconnected. They're all strung together just fine, like a pearl necklace. \n\nMy grandmother had this beautiful pearl necklace she kept in a little velvet box on her dresser. The velvet was this really deep, rich blue color, almost like the sky right after the sun goes down but before it gets completely dark. I used to love looking out the window at that time of day when I lived in my old apartment. The streetlights would just start flickering on, and they had that weird orange glow. It always made the shadows look super long and creepy on the sidewalk. \n\nI tripped on a crack in the sidewalk once when I was walking to the corner store to buy milk. I scraped my knee so bad it bled through my jeans. They were my favorite jeans, too, the ones with the little rhinestones on the back pockets. I tried to wash the blood out with cold water and soap, but it just left this ugly brown stain. Stains are impossible to get out sometimes, especially coffee. I spilled coffee on a library book once, and they made me pay for the whole thing. It was a book about gardening, which is funny because I don't even have a garden anymore. I just have a couple of sad-looking houseplants that I always forget to water. \n\nSo, no, my thoughts are perfectly connected. I don't know why you'd think otherwise.\n\nDoctor: Do you still believe that your family is hiding money from you?\n\nPatient: Yes, of course they are! I've known it for over a year now. They keep showing me these bank papers, trying to prove nothing was taken, but I know they're hiding my money. They use all these fake bills, and these so-called fundraisers, and plans for trips to cover it all up. I just have to keep confronting them until they finally tell the truth. \n\nIt's just like when my cousin said she was raising money for the animal shelter. She had these little flyers printed out on this bright pink paper. It was the exact same color as the frosting on the birthday cake I had when I turned ten. My mom made it from scratch, and it was strawberry flavored. I remember because I ate so much of it I got a terrible stomach ache and had to lay on the couch all afternoon watching cartoons. \n\nWe only had like three channels back then, and the reception was always fuzzy unless you stood right next to the TV and held the antenna just right. My brother used to make me stand there for an hour while he watched his wrestling shows. He loved wrestling. He had all these action figures he'd smash together on the living room rug. It was a shag rug, kind of a mustard yellow color, and it was impossible to vacuum. You'd always find little plastic weapons or pennies buried deep in the fibers. \n\nPennies are so useless now anyway. You can't even buy a piece of candy for a penny anymore. I remember when you could go to the corner store and get those little Swedish Fish for a penny a piece. They were so chewy they'd get stuck in your teeth for hours. You'd have to use a toothpick to dig them out, but if you pushed too hard, your gums would bleed. \n\nBut yeah, I know they're hiding it, and I won't stop until they admit it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Jumping from idea to idea? No, my mind doesn't do that. I always finish what I'm thinking about. I'm very thorough, like someone painting a fence. 

My dad painted our fence white when I was little, but he used this really cheap paint that started peeling off in big strips after just one winter. The strips looked like those little white ribbons they tie around the stems of wedding bouquets. I went to a wedding once, my second cousin's I think, and the food was just awful. They served this dry, rubbery chicken with a weird yellow sauce on top. It tasted exactly like the time I accidentally licked a lemon-scented marker in second grade. 

We had this big box of markers in class, but half of them were dried out or the tips were pushed all the way in. The teacher, Mrs. Gable, she had this giant beehive hairdo that smelled like hairspray and peppermint. She always kept a bowl of peppermints on her desk, the ones wrapped in the crinkly clear plastic. The wrappers were so loud if you tried to open one during quiet reading time. I always got in trouble for reading too slow anyway. The words just kind of swim around on the page if I look at them too long. 

But no, my ideas don't jump around. I finish every thought I start.

He used to keep a whole jar of them on his desk, but he never actually shared them with anybody. Which is pretty rude, if you ask me. They keep shoving those bank papers in my face, acting like that proves nothing was taken, but I know they're just using all those excuses—the extra bills, the school fundraisers, the trips they're planning—to cover up that they're hiding my money. \n\nSpeaking of trips, my aunt went to Hawaii once and brought back these macadamia nuts covered in chocolate. They were okay, I guess, but I really don't like nuts in my chocolate. It messes up the texture. It's like when people put raisins in cookies and you think it's chocolate chips until you bite into it and it's all squishy and disappointing. But the teacher made me play the triangle instead because all the flutes were taken. Which is just stupid, you know? Anyone can hit a piece of metal with a little stick. It's not even real music. My brother had a whole drum set in our basement growing up, and he would bang on it all night long. It drove my mom absolutely crazy. I'm in total control of what I think about. It's not like a race car or anything. \n\nSpeaking of race cars, my uncle used to take me to the dirt track races on Friday nights when I was a kid. It was so loud you could feel it in your chest, and the dirt would get all up in your teeth. We always got those big funnel cakes with the powdered sugar, but you had to eat them fast before the wind blew the sugar all over your clothes. I wore this black t-shirt once with a picture of a wolf howling at the moon, and it got completely covered in white powder. I looked like a ghost. \n\nI saw a ghost show on TV last week where they go into abandoned hospitals with those little green cameras. Just swiped right at my hand for no reason. \n\nI don't really like cats. Dogs are better. They actually listen to you. My neighbor has a golden retriever that barks at the mailman every single day at exactly 2:00 PM. You can practically set your watch by it.
